11.7.4 MMH Key Derivation for CMTS Extended MIC
The CMTS MUST derive the MMH key for the CMTS extended MIC as defined in this section. The CMTS and the
backend provisioning system share a secret key, κ CMTS − EMIC . This is equivalent to the shared secret S in Section
11.7.3.
From κ CMTS − EMIC , the CMTS derives two shared secrets, equivalent to S1 and S 2 in Section 11.7.3:

                                             S1 = S [0]  S [2]  S [4]  ...  S [n − 2]
                                             S2 = S [1]  S [3]  S [5]  ...  S [n − 1]

where S[i] is the ith octet of S (with respect to 0) and S contains n octets.
The CMTS uses the ASCII-encoded string "CMTS-EMIC" as the value of ∆ 1 in the calculation of the MMH-MAC.

The CMTS uses the ASCII-encoded string "CMTS-EMIC-PAD" as the value of ∆ 2 in the calculation of the MMH-
MAC.
The CMTS uses the function F as defined in Section 11.7.6 as the pseudo-random number generator when
calculating the MMH-MAC.

11.7.5 Shared Secret Recommendations
Though this specification emplaces no limitations or requirements on the length or contents of the secret that is
shared between the backend provisioning system and the CMTS, it assumes that the secret contains sufficient
entropy to ensure adequate cryptographic security when the secret is used in the cryptographic calculations
contained in this document. In order to meet this assumption, it is recommended that the shared secret:
      1.   be a pseudo-random binary value (as opposed to ASCII or some other simple encoding system that encodes
           certain bit positions to predictable values), and
      2.   have a length of at least 16 octets.
In the event that the output of a hash function over some simple ASCII-encoded message is used as the shared secret
(which is not recommended), the input message should contain at least 160 characters.

11.7.6 Key Generation Function
Key derivation sections in this document refer to a function F(S, seed) where S is a shared secret from which keying
material is derived, and seed is a constant string. The output of F() is a pseudo-random sequence suitable for use as a
key. The output of F(S, seed) is generated as follows:
      1.   From S, generate a derived shared secret, S′, by accumulation as follows: the value S′ is obtained by
           XORing every 16 octets of S, padding with zeroes as necessary (i.e., add zeroes to the end of S to pad it out
           to a length that is an integral multiple of 16 octets).
      2.   Use S′ as the initial key to the AES-128 (i.e., 128-bit key, 128-bit block) algorithm operating in counter
           (CTR) mode.
      3.   For each block of output needed, set the IV equal to the value of the seed, truncated to 128 bits or zero-
           extended to 128 bits as necessary, and XORed with the number of the block, starting with 1 (one) and
           incrementing by 1 (one) for each block processed.
The output of F(S, seed) is the concatenation of the blocks of output obtained in step 3. Any unused octets at the end
of the last repetition of step 3 are discarded.




116                                                      CableLabs®                                            09/03/25
Security Specification                                                               CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903


12 PHYSICAL PROTECTION OF KEYS AND SECURITY PARAMETERS
   WITHIN THE CM
12.1 Key Storage
CMs MUST store and maintain the CM device certificate RSA private/public key pairs.
The CM MUST store the CM device certificate private keys in a manner that deters unauthorized disclosure and
modification.
A CM SHOULD prevent debugger tools from reading the CM device certificate private key in production devices
by restricting or blocking physical access to memory containing this key.
The CM MUST meet [FIPS 140-2] security requirements for all instances of private and public permanent key storage.
The CM MUST meet [FIPS 140-2] Security Level 1. [FIPS 140-2] Security Level 1 requires minimal physical
protection through the use of production-grade enclosures. The reader should refer to the cited document for the
formal requirements; however, below is a summary of those requirements.
Under the [FIPS 140-2] classification of "physical embodiments" of cryptographic modules, external CMs are
"multiple-chip stand-alone cryptographic modules." [FIPS 140-2] specifies the following Security Level 1
requirements for multiple-chip standalone modules.
    •    The chips are to be of production-grade quality, which includes standard passivation techniques (i.e., a
         sealing coat over the chip circuitry to protect it against environmental or other physical damage).
    •   The circuitry within the module is to be implemented as a production-grade multiple-chip embodiment (i.e.,
        an IC printed circuit board, a ceramic substrate, etc.).
    •   The module is to be entirely contained within a metal or hard plastic production-grade enclosure, which
        may include doors or removable covers.
An internal CM (defined in [DOCSIS CMCIv3.0]) would be classified as a [FIPS 140-2] "multiple-chip embedded
cryptographic module." The Security Level 1 requirements for these devices are contained in the first two bullets
above.

12.2 Persistent Security Attribute Values
The CM MUST store PSA values in a manner that deters unauthorized disclosure and modification.
CMs SHOULD prevent debugger tools from reading PSA values in production devices by restricting or blocking
physical access to memory containing these values.
The CM MUST store PSA values such that unauthorized modifications are detected using either a symmetric keyed
hash algorithm which is equivalent to or stronger than HMAC-SHA256 or using an asymmetric digital signature
algorithm which is equivalent to or stronger than 2048-bit RSA.
The CM MUST provide integrity protection to all PSA values stored on the device.




09/03/25                                           CableLabs®                                                       117
CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903                                          Data-Over-Cable Service Interface Specifications


13 BPI+ X.509 CERTIFICATE PROFILE AND MANAGEMENT
DOCSIS technology employs [X.509] Version 3 digital certificates for authenticating key exchanges between CM
and CMTS (see [X.509]). [X.509] is a general-purpose standard; the DOCSIS certificate profile, described here,
further specifies the contents of the certificate's defined fields. This certificate profile also defines the hierarchy of
trust for the management and validation of DOCSIS certificates.
Except where otherwise noted in Appendix III, DOCSIS certificates comply with [RFC 5280].
The DOCSIS certificate profile draws extensively from the Secure Electronic Transaction (SET) system (see [SET
Book 2]). The overall organization of this section, as well as some of the section's contents, reflect that system.

13.1 BPI+ Certificate Management Architecture Overview
The DOCSIS certificate management architecture for BPI+ CM authentication consists of two public key
infrastructures (PKIs): a 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI to support backward compatibility with devices implementing older
versions of DOCSIS technology and a 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI that provides stronger cryptography algorithms and
key sizes. The 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI is defined by the DOCSIS 3.0 Security Specification [DOCSIS SECv3.0]. The
2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI was first defined in [DOCSIS SECv3.1] and is carried forward into this specification. The
2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI consists of a three-level hierarchy of trust supporting three types of certificates:
      •   root CA certificate,
      •   device CA certificate, and
      •   CM device certificates.
The root CA certificate is used as a trust anchor for the PKI and issues the device CA certificate, which issues the
CM device certificates. The 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI uses a "centralized" model where the device CA is hosted by
CableLabs or an approved third party that issues CM device certificates to approved manufacturers. CableLabs
manages the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI and the certificates issued from its CAs (CableLabs root CA and CableLabs
device CA, see Appendix III). The 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI is also managed by CableLabs.
The root CA will also be used as a trust anchor for issuing and validating CA and code verification certificates
(CVCs) for the secure software download (SSD) process specified in Section 14.
The root CA generates and distributes to operators a certificate revocation list (CRL), identifying revoked
manufacturer certificates. The manner in which CRLs are distributed is outside the scope of this specification. In
order to reduce the burden on CM devices that are designed to work in multiple geographic regions, an effort will be
made to consolidate the DOCSIS 3.1 PKI hierarchy such that the same BPI+ device certificate for DOCSIS 3.1 will
also be valid for EuroDOCSIS 3.1 and other international versions of DOCSIS 3.1 and above.
There are situations where an operator would need CM devices to authenticate to a cloud server outside of BPI+ to
enable some services on those devices.
The CM MAY support the use of the CM device certificate to authenticate the compliant DOCSIS CM device to a
cloud server.
The same requirements for physical protection of the private keys as defined in Section 12 would apply to this use
case as well.
The CM MAY support additional CA certificates for the purpose of authenticating the identity of a cloud server.
The authentication mechanism is outside the scope of this specification.
The CM MUST NOT expose the DOCSIS private key to any party when using a non-DOCSIS trust anchor to
authenticate its identity to a cloud server.
When the server side is deployed within the operators' network, the use of DOCSIS certificates is allowed. In this
configuration, no change is needed to the trust store in the DOCSIS device, and applications can rely on the
DOCSIS trust store in the device. The CM can use a non-DOCSIS trust store, including a non-DOCSIS root, to
authenticate the identity of servers. The authentication mechanism is outside the scope of this specification. How the
CM is securely provisioned with this non-DOCSIS trust store is outside the scope of this specification. For example,
the CM might include the additional CA certificates in a software image.


118                                                   CableLabs®                                                  09/03/25
Security Specification                                                                  CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903


The CM MUST store non-DOCSIS trust anchors in a trust store separate from the DOCSIS trust store.
The CM MUST implement the same level of security for the non-DOCSIS trust store it uses for the DOCSIS trust
store.
The use of strong cryptographic algorithm to secure the communication channel against eavesdropping (i.e.,
AES-128 or more secure) is required. The use of client-side authentication without proper server authentication is
prohibited.
The CM MUST provide either an equivalent or higher level of security to standard protocols widely adopted over
either the Internet or in the broadband community.
The CM MUST use TLS v1.2 or newer when authenticating with a cloud server.

13.2 Cable Modem Certificate Storage and Management in the CM
The CM MUST have two factory-installed CM device certificates (and their associated private keys).
The CM MUST have a CM device certificate installed that is issued from the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI.
The CM MUST have a CM device certificate installed that is issued from the 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI.
The CM MUST have the same RSA public key in the CM device certificate as the RSA public key in the BPKM
attributes depending upon which CM device certificate is used for authentication.
The CM MUST use the CM device certificate issued from the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI when authenticating with a
DOCSIS 3.1 or higher CMTS.
If a certificate from the 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI is installed, the CM MUST use the CM device certificate issued from
the 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI when authenticating with a DOCSIS 3.0 or older version of DOCSIS CMTS.
The CM's non-volatile memory MUST contain a root CA certificate for SSD image verification.
The CM's non-volatile memory MUST have two CA certificates (the device CA certificate from the 2nd GEN
DOCSIS PKI and the manufacturer CA certificate from the 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI) that signed the CM device
certificates.
The CM MUST use the device CA certificate issued from the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI when authenticating with a
DOCSIS 3.1 or higher CMTS and use the manufacturer CA certificate issued from the 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI
otherwise.
The CM MAY be capable of updating or replacing the device CA certificate or the 1st GEN DOCSIS manufacturer
CA certificate via the DOCSIS code download file (see Section 14, Secure Software Download (SSD)).
The CM MAY embed device CA or 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI manufacturer CA certificates.
The CM receives device CA certificates and CMTS device certificates through either the CM's provisioning
interface or through receipt and processing the Message-Signature TLV of the Authorization Reply messages.
The CM MUST be able to process certificate serial number values containing 20 octets or fewer.
The CM MUST accept certificates that have serial numbers that are negative or zero.

13.3 CM Validation of CMTS and CA Certificates
The CM validates the certification paths of CA and CMTS device certificates using basic path validation rules
defined in [RFC 5280] and the criteria below.
The CM MUST label CA and CMTS certificates as Invalid if their certification paths are invalid.
The CM MUST store root certificates that enable trusted chaining in the CM trust anchor store (see Section 15.1.3).
The CM MUST mark a chained certificate as Valid only if:
    1.   the certificate chains to a root CA, trusted, or valid certificate that has not been revoked as defined by the
         Basic Path Validation section in [RFC 5280] and specified in Section 7.1.1.2.2; and
    2.   if and only if the current time has been acquired on the CM, the current time falls within the validity period
         of each chained or root certificate within the certificate chain; and


09/03/25                                             CableLabs®                                                      119
CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903                                           Data-Over-Cable Service Interface Specifications


      3.   the certificate is not identified as revoked (see Section 7.1.1.2.2).
The CM MUST NOT invalidate certificates that have non-specified critical extensions (contrary to [RFC 5280]) if
the certificates satisfy the validity criteria above.
If time-of-day has not been acquired and certificate validation needs to be performed on the CM, the CM MUST
delay the checking of the validity periods of the CMTS certificate and revocation information after time-of-day
acquisition.
In case the certificate chain validation fails, the CM SHOULD reset the TEK state machine and transition the
authorization state machine to the [Silent] state with a CMTS Reject event (see Sections 7.1.6 and 7.1.6.3.4).

13.4 Certificate Processing and Management in the CMTS
BPKM employs digital certificates to allow CMTSs to verify the binding between a CM's identity (encoded in a digital
certificate's subject name) and its public key. The CMTS does this by validating the CM device certificate's
certification path. This path will typically consist of three chained certificates: the CM device certificate, the device
CA certificate (MFG CA certificate for 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI), and the root CA certificate (see Section 13.1).
Validating the chain follows the "basic path validation" rules defined in [RFC 5280].
The CMTS MUST support validating certificate chains from the DOCSIS 2nd Gen PKI defined in the Trust
Infrastructure document [C-PKI-TI].
[RFC 4131] requires that CMTSs support administrative controls that allow the operator to override certification
chain validation by identifying a particular CA or CM device certificate as trusted or untrusted. This section
specifies the management model for the exercise of these controls, as well as the processing a CMTS undertakes to
assess a CM device certificate's validity, and thus verify the binding between the CM's identity and its public key.
The CMTS MUST be able to process certificate serialNumber values containing 20 octets or fewer.
The CMTS MUST accept certificates that have serial numbers that are negative or zero.
With the expiration of DOCSIS backward compatible PKI MFG CA certificates beginning in May 2021, some
manufacturers have requested and received MFG CA certificates with extended validity periods. These MFG CA
certificates with extended validity periods differ from the original MFG CA certificates only in the following
attributes.
      1.   Certificate expiration date
      2.   Certificate signature
      3.   Certificate serial number
Because the two MFG CA certificates, the original one and the one with extended validity period, have different
attributes, they are considered different and unique certificates. The following requirements are to clarify that all
unique MFG CA certificates are required to be retained by the CMTS and the CMTS independently applies
administrative controls to each unique certificate.
The CMTS MUST retain in the CMTS CA cert table all unique MFG CA certificates learned through cable modem
authentication messages.
The CMTS MUST independently apply administrative controls to all unique MFG CA certificates in the CMTS CA
cert table.
Appendix III describes the format of the subject name field for each type of DOCSIS certificate. The issuer field of a
certificate matches exactly the subject field of the issuing certificate. 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI certificates transmitted
by a CM in an Auth Info or Auth Request message have name fields that conform to the format described in Appendix
III.
A CMTS MUST be capable of processing the name fields of a certificate if the name fields conform to the indicated
format in Appendix III.
A CMTS MAY choose to accept a certificate that has name fields that do not conform to the indicated format in
Appendix III.



120                                                     CableLabs®                                             09/03/25
Security Specification                                                                  CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903


The CMTS MUST process certificate extensions as defined by [RFC 5280] (see Appendix III for certificate profile
and extension definitions).

13.4.1 CMTS Certificate Management Model
The CMTS holds copies of the root CA, device CA (MFG CA for 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI), and CM device
certificates (see Section 13.1), which it obtains through either provisioning or BPKM messaging. Each certificate
learned by a CMTS MUST be assigned one of four states: Untrusted, Trusted, Chained, or Root.
The CMTS MUST have a CMTS device certificate installed that is issued from the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI.
The CMTS MUST support the ability to provision at least two root CA certificates.
The CMTS MUST have at least one root CA installed that is issued from the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI.
The CMTS MUST have at least one root CA installed that is issued from the 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI.
SECv4_0-REQ-411The CMTS MUST support the ability to display the entire root certificate(s) and/or its
thumbprint to the operator.
The CMTS MUST have a device CA installed that is issued from the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI. This device CA is the
issuer of the CMTS device certificates and is sent to the DOCSIS 4.0 CM through CMTS's Authorization Reply
message during the BPI+V2 process.
A CMTS learns of device CA certificates through either the CMTS's provisioning interface or through receipt and
processing of client CMs' Authentication Information messages.
The CMTS MUST use learned device CA certificates issued from the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI or manufacturing CA
for 1st GEN DOCSIS PKI when validating the received CM device certificates.
Regardless of how a CMTS obtains its device CA certificates, the CMTS MUST mark them as either Untrusted,
Trusted, or Chained.
If a CA certificate is not self-signed, the CMTS MUST mark the certificate as Chained.
The CMTS, however, MUST support administrative controls that allow an operator to override the Chained marking
and identify a given CA certificate as Trusted or Untrusted.
If a device CA certificate is self-signed, the CMTS MUST mark the certificate as either Trusted or Untrusted,
according to administratively controlled CMTS policy.
A CMTS obtains copies of CM device certificates in the Authorization Requests it receives from CMs. CM device
certificates are issued by a device CA. Thus, the CMTS MUST mark CM device certificates as Chained unless
overridden by CMTS administrative control and configured as Trusted or Untrusted.

13.4.2 CMTS Validation of CA Certificates and CM Certificates
The CMTS validates the certification paths of CA and CM device certificates using basic path validation rules
defined in [RFC 5280] and the criteria below.
The CMTS MUST label CA and cable modem certificates as Valid or Invalid if their certification paths are valid or
invalid, respectively.
Trusted certificates, provisioned in the CMTS, MUST be Valid; this is true even if the current time does not fall
within the trusted certificate's validity period.
Untrusted certificates, provisioned in the CMTS, MUST be Invalid.
The CMTS MUST mark a chained certificate as Valid only if:
    1.   the certificate chains to a root CA, trusted, or valid certificate that has not been revoked as defined by the
         Basic Path Validation section in [RFC 5280]; and
    2.   the current time falls within the validity period of each chained or root certificate within the certificate
         chain; and




09/03/25                                             CableLabs®                                                         121
CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903                                          Data-Over-Cable Service Interface Specifications


      3.   the certificate is not identified as revoked (see Section 13.5, Certificate Revocation); and
      4.   the CM MAC address encoded in the CM device certificate's tbsCertificate.subject field matches the CM
           MAC address. In BPI+ V1, the CM certificate is carried in the CM-Certificate TLV, while in BPI+
           V2, the CM certificate is carried in the Message-Signature TLV of the Auth Request message; and
      5.   for BPI+ V1 only, the RSA public key encoded in the Authorization Request's BPKM attributes matches
           the RSA public key encoded in CM device certificate's tbsCertificate.subjectPublicKeyInfo field; and
      6.   in the case of a CM device certificate, if the KeyUsage extension is present, the digitalSignature and/or
           keyAgreement bits are turned on, the keyEncipherment bit is turned on, and the keyCertSign and cRLSign bits
           are off; in the case of a device CA certificate, if the KeyUsage extension is present, the keyCertSign bit is
           turned on.
The CMTS MUST implement an administrative control option to ignore when the current time falls within the
validity period of each chained or root certificate within the certificate chain.
If validity period checking is enabled and the time of day has not been acquired by the CMTS, a (non-permanent)
Authorization Reject message MUST be returned by the CMTS in response to an Authorization Request.
The CMTS MUST NOT invalidate certificates that have non-specified critical extensions (contrary to [RFC 5280])
as long as the certificates satisfy the validity criteria above.

13.5 Certificate Revocation
Providing a mechanism for certificate revocation is a normal part of PKI management. When a certificate is issued,
it is expected to be in use for its entire validity period. However, various circumstances may cause a certificate to
become invalid prior to the expiration of the validity period. Such circumstances include change of name, change of
association between subject and CA, and compromise or suspected compromise of the corresponding private key.
Under such circumstances, the CA needs to revoke the certificate. Two methods of supporting certificate revocation
are defined in this specification: certificate revocation lists (CRLs) and Online Certificate Status Protocol (OCSP).
The CMTS MUST support configuration of none, one, or both certificate revocation methods to be enabled at the
same time.
Please refer to Section 7.1.1.2 for more details on message validation and OCSP responses processing in BPI+V2.

13.5.1 Certificate Revocation Lists
[RFC 5280] defines a method for revoking certificates using [X.509] certificate revocation lists (CRLs).
Figure 14 shows a framework for managing and distributing CRLs. A CRL is a digitally signed, timestamped list of
certificate serial numbers revoked by a certificate authority (CA). When a CA identifies the compromised
certificates, the CA could generate the CRL itself, or a CA could delegate the CRL generation to a third-party CRL
issuer. The CRL repository is a system that maintains a database of revoked certificates. The interface between the
CA or CRL issuer and the CRL repository is outside the scope of this specification.


                           CA

                                                       CRL
                                                                                         CMTS
                                                     Repository

                       CRL Issuer


                                               Figure 14 - CRL Framework


The CMTS retrieves CRL entries from the CRL repository and uses this information to verify if a certificate
received during the CM registration process is revoked.




122                                                   CableLabs®                                              09/03/25
Security Specification                                                                CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903


13.5.1.1 CMTS CRL Support
The CMTS MAY support CM certificate revocation via certificate revocation lists.
The CMTS requirements in this section are conditional upon the CMTS supporting CRL for CM certificate
revocation.
The CMTS MUST support retrieval of CRL files formatted as defined in [RFC 5280]. CRL files may identify
revoked certificates that were issued from different CAs.
The CMTS MUST support extensions related to indirect CRL files as defined in [RFC 5280].
The CMTS MUST support HTTP as defined in [RFC 2616] for downloading CRL files.
Before using the information in a CRL file, the CMTS MUST verify that its digital signature chains to a trusted root
CA. Trusted root CAs are administratively provisioned in the CMTS.
If the CRL file digital signature cannot be verified, the CMTS MUST discard the CRL file.
The CMTS MUST validate if a CA certificate or CM device certificate is revoked during the certificate validation
process specified in Section 13.4.2, CMTS Validation of CA Certificates and CM Certificates.
If the CRL contains the nextUpdate value, the CMTS MUST refresh the CRL after the specified time has passed.
If the CMTS fails to retrieve the new CRL, the CMTS MUST log an event [DOCSIS CCAP-OSSIv4.0] and
continue to use its current CRL. If the CMTS fails to retrieve the new CRL, it should attempt to retry retrieval of the
CRL file on a periodic basis.
If the CRL does not contain the nextUpdate value, the CMTS MUST refresh the CRL according to the configured
value as defined in [DOCSIS CCAP-OSSIv4.0].
When the CMTS is configured to use a CRL, the CMTS MUST attempt to retrieve the CRL file each time it starts
up. During CMTS startup, it is possible that some CMs may perform BPI+ authorization before the CRL file has
been retrieved.
When the CMTS is configured to use a CRL and a CM's device certificate chain is validated during CMTS startup
before the CRL file is retrieved, the CMTS MUST log an event for that CM [DOCSIS CCAP-OSSIv4.0] and bypass
CRL checking.

13.5.2 Online Certificate Status Protocol
[RFC 6960] defines an Online Certificate Status Protocol (OCSP) for querying the status of a digital certificate. The
CMTS sends a certificate status request to an OCSP responder when it receives a CA certificate or a CM device
certificate (see Figure 15). The OCSP responder sends a status response indicating that the certificate is "good,"
"revoked," or "unknown." The OCSP responder checks only the revocation status of a certificate; it does not verify
the validity of the certificate itself. The CMTS uses the result from the OCSP responder during certificate validation
process specified in Section 13.4.2.




                                           Figure 15 - OCSP Framework


The CMTS MAY be capable of acting as an OCSP client for CM certificate revocation checks.
The CMTS requirements listed in this section are conditional upon the CMTS supporting an OCSP client for CM
certificate revocation.
The CMTS MUST be capable of acting as an OCSP client as defined in [RFC 6960].
The CMTS SHOULD cache the OCSP response status for a certificate if the nextUpdate value is present in the
OCSP response.
If the CMTS caches the OCSP response status for a given certificate, the CMTS MUST retrieve the revocation
status from the cache.


09/03/25                                            CableLabs®                                                     123
CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903                                       Data-Over-Cable Service Interface Specifications


Once the nextUpdate time for that certificate has passed, the CMTS MUST continue using the revocation status
value from the cache until an update is retrieved from the OCSP responder.
If the CMTS is unable to retrieve the OCSP status for an uncached certificate or if the retrieved status is "unknown",
the CMTS MUST log an event (see [DOCSIS CCAP-OSSIv4.0]) and assume the certificate status to be "good".
If the nextUpdate value is not present in the OCSP response, the CMTS MUST NOT cache the OCSP response
status for a certificate.
If the CMTS is configured with OCSP responder information, the CMTS MUST send an OCSP request when a CA
certificate or CM device certificate is obtained using the Authentication Information message or Authentication
Request message, respectively, unless there is a valid certificate status in the cache.
When the CMTS is attempting to communicate with the OCSP responder, the exchange is not to significantly delay
the CM provisioning process.
If no OCSP response is received, the CMTS MUST proceed using the currently cached revocation status.
For uncached certificate states, the CMTS MUST proceed as if an OCSP response with the status "good" has been
received.
The CMTS MUST support OCSP over HTTP as described in [RFC 6960].
The CMTS MAY generate a signature in the OCSP request.
The CMTS MUST bypass validation of the signature in an OCSP response based on the configured value as defined
in [DOCSIS CCAP-OSSIv4.0].




124                                                 CableLabs®                                              09/03/25
Security Specification                                                                 CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903


14 SECURE SOFTWARE DOWNLOAD (SSD)
14.1 Introduction
DOCSIS technology supports downloading code to CMs. Authenticating the source and verifying the integrity of
downloaded code is vital to the overall operation and security of DOCSIS-based networks. Code is signed with a
certificate from the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI (see Section 13.1) and then validated by the CM.
The software download module is an attractive target for an attacker. If an attacker were able to mount a scalable
attack against the software download module, they could potentially install code to disable all the CMs within a
domain or disrupt service on a wide scale. To thwart these attacks, the attacker is forced to overcome several
security barriers.

14.2 Overview
The requirements defined in this section address these primary security goals for the code download process.
    •    The CM needs to have a means to authenticate that the originator of any download code is a known and
         trusted source.
    •    The CM needs to have a means to verify that the downloaded code has not been altered from the original
         form in which it was provided by the trusted source.
    •    The process needs to strive to simplify the operator's code file-handling requirements and provide
         mechanisms for the operator to upgrade or downgrade the code version of cable modems on their network.
    •    The process allows operators to dictate and control their policies with respect to: (a) which code files will
         be accepted by CMs within their network and (b) security controls that define the security of the process on
         their network.
    •    CMs are able to move freely among systems controlled by different operators.
    •    Support updating the root CA certificate in the CM (optional).
    •    Support updating the device CA certificate in the CM (optional).
    •    Support updating the legacy root CA public key in the CM (optional).
    •    Support updating the legacy manufacturer CA certificate in the CM (optional).
This document limits its scope to these primary system security requirements but acknowledges that in some cases
additional security may be desired. The concerns of individual operators or CM manufacturers may result in
additional security related to the distribution or installation of code into a CM or other DOCSIS network element.
This specification does not restrict the use of further protections as long as they do not conflict with the requirements
of this specification.
Multiple levels of protection are required to protect and verify the code download.
    •    The manufacturer of the CM code always applies a digital signature to the code file. The signature is
         verified with a certificate chain that extends up to the root CA. The manufacturer signature affirms the
         source and integrity of the code file to the CM.
    •    Though the manufacturer always signs its code file, an operator may later apply its code signature in
         addition to the manufacturer signature. If a second signature is present, the CM verifies both signatures
         with a certificate chain that extends up to the root CA before accepting a code file.
    •    OSS mechanisms for the provisioning and control of the CM are important to the proper execution of this
         process. The code-upgrade capability of a CM is enabled during the provisioning and registration process.
         Code downloads are initiated during the provisioning and registration process or can be initiated in normal
         operation using an SNMP command.




09/03/25                                             CableLabs®                                                      125
CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903                                         Data-Over-Cable Service Interface Specifications


The code file is built using a PKCS#7-compliant structure that is defined below and in [PKCS#7]. This data
structure is referred to as the firmware authentication header (FWAH). Included in this structure are:
      •   the code image; i.e., the upgrade code image;
      •   the code verification signature (CVS); i.e., the digital signature over the code image and any other
          authenticated attributes as defined in the structure; and
      •   the code verification certificate (CVC); i.e., an [X.509]-compliant certificate that is used to deliver and
          validate the public code verification key that will verify the signature over the code image. The DOCSIS
          certificate authority, a trusted party whose public key is already stored in the CM, signs this certificate.
Figure 16 shows the basic steps required for the signing of a code image when the code file is signed only by the
CM manufacturer and when the code file is signed by the CM manufacturer and co-signed by an operator.
With DOCSIS technology, the root CA certificate is installed in each CM as a trust anchor. The code manufacturer
builds the code file by signing the code image using a DOCSIS PKCS#7 digital signature structure with a
manufacturer CVC certificate and the issuing CVC CA certificate. The code file is then sent to the operator.
The operator verifies that the code file is from a trusted DOCSIS manufacturer and has not been modified. At this
point, the operator has the option of loading the code file on the software download server as is or of adding its
signature and operator CVC and issuing CVC CA certificate to the code file. During the code upgrade process, the
CM will retrieve the code file from the software download server and verify the new code image using the root CA
certificate trust anchor before installing it (see Appendix III for CVC chain details).




126                                                  CableLabs®                                                  09/03/25
Security Specification                                                           CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903

14.3 Software Code Upgrade Requirements
The following sections define requirements of the CM software code upgrade verification process. All code
upgrades are prepared and verified as described. The 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI used for issuing CVCs consists of
three types of certificates: a root CA, the CVC CA, and the CVC. CableLabs manages the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI
and the certificates issued from its CAs (CableLabs root CA and CableLabs CVC CA; see Appendix III for
certificate profile and extension definitions).
NOTE: The CableLabs root CA is used to issue both CM device certificates and CVC certificates.
A CM MUST verify code upgrades according to this specification regardless of whether Baseline Privacy is enabled
or disabled.
The CM MUST process CVC extensions as defined by [RFC 5280].

14.3.1 Code File Processing Requirements
The code file format is defined in Appendix III.
The CM MUST reject the DOCSIS code file if the signedData field does not match the DER encoded structure
represented in Appendix III.
The CM MUST be able to verify DOCSIS code file signatures that are signed using key modulus lengths of 2048,
3072, and 4096 bits. The public exponent is F4 (65537 decimal).
The CM MUST reject the CVC if it does not match the DER encoded structure represented in Appendix III.
The CM MUST NOT install the upgraded code image unless the code image has been verified as being compatible
with the CM.
If the code download and installation is successful, then the CM MUST replace its currently stored root CA
certificate with the root CA certificate in the SignedContent field, if one was present.
If the code download and installation is successful, then the CM MUST replace its currently stored device CA
certificate with the device CA certificate received in the SignedContent field, if any were present.
If the code download and installation is successful, then the CM MUST replace its currently stored legacy
manufacturer CA certificate with the legacy manufacturer CA certificate received in the SignedContent field, if
any were present.

14.3.2 Code File Access Controls
In addition to the cryptographic controls provided by the digital signature and the certificate, special control values
are included in the code file for the CM to check before it accepts a code image as valid.
The conditions placed on the values of these control parameters MUST be satisfied before the CM attempts to
validate the CVC and the CVS (see Sections 14.3.2.1, Subject Organization Names and 14.3.2.2, Time Varying
Controls).

14.3.2.1 Subject Organization Names
The CM MUST recognize up to two names that it considers a trusted code-signing agent if present in the subject
field of a code file CVC. These are:
      •   The cable modem manufacturer
          A manufacturer CVC is always included in the code file.
          The CM MUST verify that the manufacturer name in the manufacturer CVC subject field exactly matches
          one of the manufacturer names stored in the CM's non-volatile memory by the manufacturer.
          The CM MUST store the manufacturer name of the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI in non-volatile memory.




128                                                  CableLabs®                                                09/03/25
Security Specification                                                                   CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903


    •    A co-signing agent
         As described above, DOCSIS technology permits another trusted organization to co-sign code files
         destined for the CM. In most cases, this organization is the operator. The organization name of the co-
         signing agent is communicated to the CM via a co-signer CVC in the configuration file when initializing
         the CM's code verification process.
         The CM MUST verify that the co-signer organization name in the co-signer CVC subject field exactly
         matches the co-signer organization name previously received in the co-signer initialization CVC, and
         stored by the CM.

14.3.2.2 Time Varying Controls
In support of the code upgrade process, the CM MUST keep two UTC time values associated with each code-
signing agent. These values are known as codeAccessStart and cvcAccessStart.
The CM MUST store and maintain one pair of time values for the CM manufacturer signing agent.
If the CM is assigned a code co-signing agent, the CM MUST maintain a pair of time values for the code co-signing
agent.
These values are used to control code file access to the cable modem by individually controlling the validity of the
CVS and the CVC.
Stored and maintained time values in the CM MUST have a precision of one second.
Stored and maintained time values in the CM MUST be capable of representing all times (with one second
precision) between midnight, Jan 1 1950, and midnight Jan 1 2050.
The CM MUST NOT allow the values of codeAccessStart and cvcAccessStart corresponding to the cable
modem's manufacturer signing agent to decrease.
The CM MUST NOT allow the value of codeAccessStart and cvcAccessStart corresponding to the co-
signing agent to decrease as long as the co-signing agent does not change and the CM maintains the co-signer time-
varying control values (see Section 14.3.3, Cable Modem Code Upgrade Initialization).
For the manufacturer code signing agent, the CM MUST store and maintain time-varying control values for the 2nd
GEN DOCSIS PKI in non-volatile memory.

14.3.3 Cable Modem Code Upgrade Initialization
Before the cable modem can upgrade code, it should be properly initialized. Its manufacturer first initializes the
cable modem.
Every time a CM registers on a DOCSIS network, the CM MUST check its current initialization state with respect to
the operational needs of the particular network.
It may be necessary for the cable modem to reinitialize at registration, particularly if the cable modem has moved
from one network to another.

14.3.3.1 Manufacturer Initialization
It is the responsibility of the manufacturer to install the initial code version in the CM.
In support of code upgrade verification, the CM MUST have values for the following parameters loaded into the
CM's non-volatile memory.
    •    CM manufacturer organizationName
    •    codeAccessStart initialization value

    •    cvcAccessStart initialization value
The CM MUST initialize the values of codeAccessStart and cvcAccessStart for the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI
to a UTCTime equal to the validity start time of the manufacturer's latest CVC.


09/03/25                                              CableLabs®                                                     129
CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903                                        Data-Over-Cable Service Interface Specifications


These values will be updated periodically under normal operation via manufacturer CVCs that are received and
verified by the cable modem.

14.3.3.2 Network Initialization
The method for obtaining CM code download files is defined in [DOCSIS MULPIv4.0]. The CM receives settings
relevant to code upgrade verification in its configuration file.
The CM MUST NOT use these settings until after the CMTS has successfully registered the CM.
The configuration file normally includes the most up-to-date CVC applicable for the destination cable modem.
When the configuration file is used to initiate a code upgrade, it will include a CVC to initialize the cable modem for
accepting code files according to this specification.
Alternatively, the configuration file can directly include the FWAH of the firmware upgrade (see Table 68) for the
destination cable modem. In this case, the signing CVC and its issuing CVC CA certificates (together with the co-
signer chain, if co-signed) are directly included in the FWAH.
Regardless of whether a code upgrade is required, a CVC in the configuration file MUST be processed by the cable
modem.
A configuration file can contain:
      •   no CVCs, or
      •   the manufacturer CVC chain and/or the co-signer CVC chain from the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI (Type 81
          and 82), or
      •   the FWAH of a firmware update file that already contains the CVC chains (Type 104.1.2).
In addition to the authentication information, the configuration file can also contain:
      •   (optional) revocation information (OCSP-CVC-Responses) for the CVC chains (Type 104.1.1).
Before the CM will enable its ability to upgrade code files from the network, a CM MUST receive either a valid
CVC or a valid FWAH in a configuration file and successfully register with the CMTS.
If the CM's configuration file does not contain either a valid CVC or a valid FWAH and its ability to upgrade code
files has been disabled, then the CM MUST reject any information in either a CVC or a FWAH delivered via
SNMP.
When the cable modem's configuration file does not contain either a co-signer CVC or a FWAH that carries a co-
signer signature, the CM MUST NOT accept code files that have been co-signed.
If the CM is configured to accept code co-signed by a code-signing agent, the following parameters MUST be stored
in the CM's memory when the co-signer CVC is processed.
      •   Co-signing agent's organizationName
      •   Co-signer cvcAccessStart
      •   Co-signer codeAccessStart
Unlike the manufacturer organizationName and time varying control values, the co-signer organizationName and
time varying control values are not required to be stored in non-volatile memory.

14.3.3.2.1     Processing the Configuration File CVC
When a CVC is included in the configuration file, the CM MUST verify the CVC before accepting any of the code
upgrade settings it contains.
Upon receipt of the CVC, the CM MUST perform the following validation and procedural steps.
If any of the following verification checks fail, the CM MUST immediately halt the CVC verification process.




130                                                  CableLabs®                                              09/03/25
Security Specification                                                                CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903


If the CM configuration file does not include either a valid CVC or a valid FWAH, the CM MUST NOT download
upgrade code files, whether triggered by the CM configuration file or via an SNMP MIB.
If the CM configuration file does not include either a valid CVC or a valid FWAH, the CM SHOULD NOT process
CVCs or FWAH subsequently delivered via an SNMP MIB.
If the CM configuration file does not include either a valid CVC or a valid FWAH, the CM MUST NOT accept
information from a CVC or FWAH subsequently delivered via an SNMP MIB.
Following receipt of a CVC in a configuration file, and after the CM has successfully registered with the CMTS, the
CM MUST:
    1.   verify that the Extended Key Usage extension is present in the CVC, as specified in Appendix III;
    2.   verify that the manufacturer CVC validity start time is greater than or equal to the manufacturer
         cvcAccessStart value currently held in the CM if the CVC is a manufacturer CVC and the subject
         organizationName is identical to the CM's manufacturer name;
    3.   reject this CVC and log an error if the CVC is a manufacturer CVC and the subject organizationName is
         not identical to the cable modem's manufacturer name;
    4.   verify that the validity start time is greater than or equal to the co-signer cvcAccessStart value currently
         held in the CM if the CVC is a co -signer CVC and the subject organizationName is identical to the
         CM's current code co-signing agent;
    5.   after the CVC has been validated (and registration is complete), make this subject organization name
         become the CM's new code co-signing agent if the CVC is a co-signer CVC and the subject
         organizationName is not identical to the current code co-signing agent name;

    6.   verify that both the CVC and the CVC CA certificate signatures chain up to the root CA certificate of the
         2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI held by the CM;
    7.   if time-of-day has been acquired, verify that the validity periods for the CVC certificate, the issuing CVC
         CA certificate, and the root CA have not expired; and
    8.   update the CM's current value of cvcAccessStart corresponding to the CVC's subject
         organizationName (i.e., manufacturer or code co-signing agent) with the validity start time value from
         the validated CVC. If the validity start time value is greater than the CM's current value of
         codeAccessStart, update the CM's codeAccessStart value with the validity start time value.

14.3.3.2.2    Processing the SNMP CVC
When the CM is not enabled to upgrade code files, it MUST reject all CVCs received via SNMP. CVCs received via
SNMP will also chain up to the same root CA certificate or public key that was used to validate the CVC in the
configuration file (see Section 14.3.5).
The CM MUST process CVCs received via SNMP when it is enabled to upgrade code files.
When the CM is not enabled to upgrade code files, the CM MUST reject all CVCs received via SNMP.
When validating a CVC received via SNMP, the CM MUST perform the following validation and procedural steps.
If any of the following verification checks fail, the CM MUST immediately halt the CVC verification process, log
the error if applicable, and remove all remnants of the process up to that step.
When a CM receives a CVC via SNMP, the CM MUST:
    1.   verify that the Extended Key Usage extension is in the CVC as specified in Appendix III;
    2.   verify that the manufacturer CVC validity start time is greater than the manufacturer cvcAccessStart value
         currently held in the CM if the CVC subject organizationName is identical to the CM's manufacturer
         name;




09/03/25                                            CableLabs®                                                    131
CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903                                          Data-Over-Cable Service Interface Specifications


      3.   verify that the validity start time is greater than the co-signer cvcAccessStart value currently held in the
           CM if the CVC subject organizationName is identical to the cable modem's current code co-signing
           agent;
      4.   reject this CVC if the CVC subject organizationName is not identical to CM's manufacturer or current
           code co-signing agent name;
      5.   verify that the CVC and the CVC CA certificate signatures chain up to the root CA certificate of the 2nd
           GEN DOCSIS PKI;
      6.   if the time-of-day has been acquired, verify that the validity periods for the CVC certificate, the issuing
           CVC CA certificate, and the root CA certificate have not expired; and
      7.   update the current value of the subject's cvcAccessStart values with the validated CVC's validity start
           time value.
If the validity start time value is greater than the CM's current value of codeAccessStart, the CM MUST replace
its codeAccessStart value with the validity start value.

14.3.3.2.3      Processing the Configuration File Firmware Auth Header (FAH)
When a firmware authentication header (FWAH) is included in the configuration file via the CodeFile-AuthHeader
or Co-AH TLV (Type 99.1.1) as described in [DOCSIS MULPIv4.0], the CM MUST verify the contents of the TLV
before accepting any of the code upgrade settings it contains according to the following validation and procedural
steps.
If any of the verification checks fail, or if any section of the code file is rejected due to invalid formatting, the CM
MUST immediately halt the download process and log the error if applicable, remove all remnants of the process to
that step, and continue to operate with its existing code.
Following receipt of a FWAH in a configuration file, after the CM has successfully registered with the CMTS, and
before downloading the code file, the CM MUST validate the signing certificates (i.e., manufacturer's and, if
present, co-signer's) and their certificate chains by following the steps in Section 14.3.3.2.1, Processing the
Configuration File CVC.
Following receipt of a FWAH in a configuration file, after the CM has successfully registered with the CMTS, and
before downloading the code file, the CM MUST validate the FWAH according to the procedures specified in
Section 14.3.5.1, Cable Modem Code Verification Steps with the exception of signature validation in Step 5 and
Step 8-e.
When validating the FWAH signature, the CM MUST validate the correctness of the signature but delay matching
the signed value (i.e., the encrypted hash calculated over the signed data) with the SignedContent (see
Appendix III.7.3.2, Signed Content) until the CM has downloaded the code file and the SignedContent is
available on the CM.
If no error conditions have been detected during FWAH signature validation, the CM can proceed with the
download of the code file.
After the code file download is completed, the CM MUST verify that the signed value in the FWAH matches the
value calculated over the SignedContent of the downloaded code file.
The CM MUST verify that the FWAH provided via the configuration file matches the FWAH at the beginning of
the downloaded code file.
In case of FWAH signature validation errors, the CM MUST immediately halt the upgrade process and log the error
if applicable, remove all remnants of the process to that step, and continue to operate with its existing code.
After validating the correctness and integrity of the downloaded code file, if no errors have been detected, the CM
MUST proceed with the code installation as specified in Section 14.3.5.2, Cable Modem Code File Installation
Steps.




132                                                   CableLabs®                                                09/03/25
Security Specification                                                                 CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903


14.3.3.2.4    Processing SNMP-Delivered Firmware Authentication Header (FWAH)
When the CM is enabled to upgrade code files (e.g., via the use of a CVC certificate in the config file), the CM
MUST process the FWAH received via SNMP before downloading the code file.
If any of the verification checks fail, or if any section of the code file is rejected due to invalid formatting, the CM
MUST immediately halt the download process and log the error if applicable, remove all remnants of the process to
that step, and continue to operate with its existing code.
The CM MUST process the FWAH received via SNMP when it is enabled to upgrade code files.
When the CM is not enabled to upgrade code files, the CM MUST ignore all FWAH received via SNMP.
Following receipt of a FWAH via SNMP, after the CM has successfully registered with the CMTS, and before
downloading the code image file, the CM MUST validate the signing certificates (i.e., manufacturer's and, if present,
co-signer's) and their certificate chains by following the steps in Section 14.3.3.2.2, Processing the SNMP CVC.
Following receipt of a FWAH via SNMP, after the CM has successfully registered with the CMTS, and before
downloading the code file, the CM MUST validate the FWAH according to the procedures specified in Section
14.3.5.1, Cable Modem Code Verification Steps with the exception of signature validation in Step 5 and Step 8-e.
When validating the FWAH signature in Section 14.3.5.1, Cable Modem Code Verification Steps in Step 5 and Step
8-e, the CM MUST validate the correctness of the signature only and delay matching the signed value (i.e., the
encrypted hash calculated over the signed data) with the calculated value over the SignedContent until the CM
has downloaded the code file and the SignedContent is available on the CM.
If no error conditions have been detected during validation, the CM can proceed with the download of the code file.
After the code file download is completed, the CM MUST verify that the signed value in the FWAH matches the
value calculated over the SignedContent of the downloaded code file.
The CM MUST verify that the FWAH provided via SNMP matches the FWAH at the beginning of the downloaded
upgrade file.
In case of FWAH validation errors, the CM MUST immediately halt the upgrade process and log the error if
applicable, remove all remnants of the process to that step, and continue to operate with its existing code.
After validating the correctness and integrity of the downloaded image, if no errors have been detected, the CM
MUST proceed with the code installation as specified in Section 14.3.5.2, Cable Modem Code File Installation
Steps.

14.3.4 Code Signing Guidelines
Manufacturer and operator code signing guidelines are provided in Appendix III.

14.3.5 Code Verification Requirements
The CM MUST NOT install upgrade code unless the code has been verified.

14.3.5.1 Cable Modem Code Verification Steps
When downloading code, the CM MUST perform the verification checks presented in this section.
If any of the verification checks fail, or if any section of the code file is rejected due to invalid formatting, the CM
MUST immediately halt the download process and log the error if applicable, remove all remnants of the process to
that step, and continue to operate with its existing code.
The verification checks can be performed in any order.
    1.   The CM MUST verify that:
         a.   the value of signingTime is equal to or greater than the manufacturer codeAccessStart value
              currently held in the CM;
         b.   the value of signingTime is equal to or greater than the manufacturer CVC validity start time; and



09/03/25                                             CableLabs®                                                     133
CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903                                          Data-Over-Cable Service Interface Specifications


           c.   the value of signingTime is less than or equal to the manufacturer CVC validity end time.
      2.   The CM MUST verify that:
           a.   the manufacturer CVC subject organizationName is identical to the manufacturer name currently
                stored in the CM's memory;
           b.   the manufacturer CVC validity start time is equal to or greater than the manufacturer
                cvcAccessStart value currently held in the CM; and

           c.   the Extended Key Usage extension in the manufacturer CVC meets the requirements of Appendix III.
      3.   The CM MUST verify that the manufacturer CVC chains up to root CA held by the CM.
      4.   If the time-of-day has been acquired, the CM MUST verify that the validity periods for the CVC, the
           issuing CA certificate, and the root CA certificate have not expired.
      5.   The CM MUST verify the manufacturer code file signature. If the signature does not verify, the CM MUST
           reject all components of the code file (including the code image), and any values derived from the
           verification process should be immediately discarded.
      6.   In case the OCSP response TLV is provided in the configuration file or via SNMP SET, the CM MUST
           validate the revocation status of the certificate(s) in the chain.
      7.   If time-of-day has not been acquired, the CM MAY ignore the validity periods of the OCSP CVC
           responses during validation.
      8.   If the manufacturer signature verifies and a co-signing agent signature is required:
           a.   The CM MUST verify that:
                (1) the co-signer signature information is included in the code file;
                (2) the value of signingTime is equal to or greater than the corresponding codeAccessStart value
                    currently held in the CM;
                (3) the value of signingTime is equal to or greater than the corresponding CVC validity start time;
                    and
                (4) the value of signingTime is less than or equal to the corresponding CVC validity end time.
           b.   The CM MUST verify that:
                (1) the co-signer CVC subject organizationName is identical to the co-signer organization name
                    currently stored in the CM's memory;
                (2) the co-signer CVC validity start time is equal to or greater than the cvcAccessStart value
                    currently held in the CM for the corresponding subject organizationName; and
                (3) the Extended Key Usage extension in the co-signer CVC meets the requirements of Appendix III.
           c.   The CM MUST verify that the co-signing CVC certificate chains up to the root CA of the new CA.
           d.   If time-of-day has been acquired, verify that the validity periods for the CVC, the issuing CVC CA
                certificate, and the root CA certificate have not expired.
           e.   The CM MUST verify the co-signer code file signature.
           f.   In case the OCSP responses TLV is provided in the configuration file or via SNMP SET, the CM
                MUST validate the revocation status of the certificate(s) in the chain.
           g.   If time-of-day has not been acquired, the CM MAY ignore the validity periods of the OCSP CVC
                responses during validation.
           If no errors have been detected during the validation process, the CM can proceed with the installation of
           the code file as described in Section 14.3.5.2, Cable Modem Code File Installation Steps.




134                                                   CableLabs®                                              09/03/25
Security Specification                                                                 CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903


         If the signature does not verify, the CM MUST reject all components of the code file (including the code
         image), and any values derived from the verification process should be immediately discarded.
    9.   Once the manufacturer, and optionally the co-signer, signature has been verified, the code image can be
         trusted and installation may proceed. Before installing the code image, the CM SHOULD immediately
         discard all other components of the code file and any values derived from the verification process except
         the [PKCS#7] signingTime values and the CVC validity start values.
    10. The CM can upgrade its software by installing the code file according to [DOCSIS MULPIv4.0].
    11. If the code installation is unsuccessful, the CM MUST discard the [PKCS#7] signingTime values and
        CVC validity start values it just received in the code file. The procedure for handling this failure condition
        is specified in [DOCSIS MULPIv4.0].
    12. Once the code installation is successful, the CM MUST:
         a.   update the current value of manufacturer codeAccessStart with the [PKCS#7] signingTime value
              and
         b.   update the current value of manufacturer cvcAccessStart with the CVC validity start value.
    13. If the code installation is successful, and if the code file was co-signed, the CM MUST:
         a.   update the current value of the co-signer codeAccessStart with the [PKCS#7] signingTime value
              and
         b.   update the current value of the co-signer cvcAccessStart with the CVC validity start value.

14.3.5.2 Cable Modem Code File Installation Steps
Once the manufacturer, and optionally the co-signer, signature has been verified, the code image can be trusted and
installation can proceed. In order to proceed with the installation of the validated software, the following steps are
performed.
    1.   Before installing the code file, the CM SHOULD immediately discard all other components of the code file
         and any values derived from the verification process except the FWAH signingTime values and the
         CVC validity start values.
    2.   The CM can upgrade its software by installing the code file according to [DOCSIS MULPIv4.0].
    3.   If the code installation is unsuccessful, the CM MUST discard the FWAH signingTime values and
         CVC validity start values it just received in the code file. The procedure for handling this failure condition
         is specified in [DOCSIS MULPIv4.0].
    4.   Once the code installation is successful, the CM MUST both update the current value of manufacturer
         codeAccessStart with the FWAH signingTime value and update the current value of
         manufacturer cvcAccessStart with the CVC validity start value.
    5.   If the code installation is successful and if the code file was co-signed, the CM MUST both update the
         current value of the co-signer codeAccessStart with the FWAH signingTime value and update the
         current value of the co-signer cvcAccessStart with the CVC validity start value.

14.3.6 DOCSIS Interoperability
Images for DOCSIS 4.0 secure software download (SSD) are to be signed using certificates from the 2nd GEN
DOCSIS PKI defined in this specification. The CM supports secure software downloads using certificates from only
the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI.
If the configuration file contains a manufacturer CVC chain and/or a co-signer CVC chain, the CM MUST perform
secure software download.




09/03/25                                            CableLabs®                                                      135
CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903                                       Data-Over-Cable Service Interface Specifications


14.3.7 Error Codes
The CM MUST log the following error events when they occur during the code verification process.
DOCSIS event logging requirements and event message formats are defined in [DOCSIS CM-OSSIv4.0].
1.    Improper code file controls
      Conditions:
      a.   CVC subject organizationName for manufacturer does not match the CM's manufacturer name.
      b.   CVC subject organizationName for code co-signing agent does not match the CM's current code co-
           signing agent.
      c.   The manufacturer FWAH signingTime value is less than the codeAccessStart value currently held in
           the CM.
      d.   The manufacturer FWAH validity start time value is less than the cvcAccessStart value currently held
           in the CM.
      e.   The manufacturer CVC validity start time is less than the cvcAccessStart value currently held in the CM.
      f.   The manufacturer FWAH signingTime value is less than the CVC validity start time.
      g.   Missing or improper extended key-usage extension in the manufacturer CVC.
      h.   The co-signer FWAH signingTime value is less than the codeAccessStart value currently held in the
           CM.
      i.   The co-signer FWAH validity start time value is less than the cvcAccessStart value currently held in the
           CM.
      j.   The co-signer CVC validity start time is less than the cvcAccessStart value currently held in the CM.
      k.   The co-signer FWAH signingTime value is less than the CVC validity start time.
      l.   Missing or improper extended key-usage extension in the co-signer CVC.
2.    Code file manufacturer CVC validation failure
      Conditions:
      a.   The manufacturer CVC in the code file does not chain to the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI.
      b.   Either the manufacturer CVC certificate or the CVC CA certificate in the code file has been revoked.
3.    Code file manufacturer CVS validation failure
4.    Code file co-signer CVC validation failure
      a.   The co-signer CVC in the code file does not chain to the 2nd GEN DOCSIS PKI.
      b.   The manufacturer CVC in the code file has been revoked.
5.    Code file co-signer CVS validation failure
6.    Improper configuration file CVC format
      a.   Missing or improper key usage attribute.
7.    Configuration file CVC validation failure
8.    Improper SNMP CVC format
      Conditions:
      a.   CVC subject organizationName for manufacturer does not match the CM's manufacturer name.
      b.   CVC subject organizationName for code co-signing agent does not match the CM's current code co-signing
           agent.


136                                                   CableLabs®                                            09/03/25
Security Specification                                                                 CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903


     c.   The CVC validity start time is less than or equal to the corresponding subject's cvcAccessStart value
          currently held in the CM.
     d.   Missing or improper key usage attribute.
9.   SNMP CVC validation failure
     Conditions:
     a.   The manufacturer CVC received via SNMP does not chain to the same root CA certificate of the 2nd GEN
          DOCSIS PKI.
     b.   The co-signer CVC received via SNMP does not chain to the same root CA certificate of the 2nd GEN
          DOCSIS PKI.

14.4 Security Considerations (Informative)
The method(s) used to protect private keys are a critical factor in maintaining security. Users authorized to sign
code, i.e., manufacturers and operators who have been issued code verification certificates (CVCs) by the DOCSIS
root CA, should protect their private keys. An attacker with access to the private key of an authorized code-signing
user can create, at will, code files that are potentially acceptable to a large number of CMs.
The defense against such an attack is for the operator to revoke the certificate whose associated code-signing private
key has been learned by the attacker. To revoke a certificate, the operator delivers to each affected CM, an updated
CVC with a validity start time that is newer than that of the certificate(s) being revoked. The new CVC can be
delivered via any of the supported mechanisms: configuration file, code file, or SNMP. The new CVC implicitly
revokes all certificates whose validity start time is earlier than that of the new CVC.
To reduce the vulnerability to this type of attack, operators should regularly update the CVC in each CM at a
frequency comparable to how often the operator would update a CRL if one were available. Regular updates help
manage the time interval during which a compromised code-signing key is useful to an attacker. CVCs should also be
updated if it is suspected that a code-signing key has been compromised. To update the CVC, the user needs a CVC
whose validity start time is newer than the CVC in the CM. This implies that the DOCSIS root CA regularly issues
new CVCs to all authorized code-signing manufacturers and operators to make the CVCs available for update.
When a CM is attempting to register on the network for the first time or after being offline for an extended period, it
should receive a trusted CVC as soon as possible. This provides the CM with the opportunity to receive the most up-
to-date CVC available and deny access to CVCs that needed to be revoked since the CM last initialization. The first
opportunity for the CM to receive a trusted CVC is in its configuration file. If the configuration file does not include
a valid CVC, the CM will not request or have the ability to remotely upgrade code files. In addition, the CM will not
accept CVCs subsequently delivered via SNMP.
To mitigate the possibility of a CM receiving a previous code file via a replay attack, the code files include a signing-
time value in the firmware authentication header (FWAH) structure as described in [PKCS#7] that can be used to
indicate the time the code image was signed. When the CM receives a code file signing-time that is later than the
signing-time it last received, it will update its internal memory with this value. The CM will not accept code files
with an earlier signing-time than this internally stored value. To upgrade a CM with a new code file without denying
access to past code files, the signer may choose not to update the signing-time. In this manner, multiple code files
with the same code signing-time allow an operator to freely downgrade a CM's code image to a past version (that is,
until the CVC is updated). This has a number of advantages for the operator, but these advantages should be
weighed against the possibilities of a code file replay attack.
Without a reliable mechanism to revert back to a known good version of code, any code-update scheme, including
the one in this specification, has the weakness that a single, successful forced update of an invalid code image may
render the CM useless or may cause the CM to behave in a manner harmful to the network. Such a CM may not be
repairable via a remote code update since the invalid code image may not support the update scheme.




09/03/25                                             CableLabs®                                                      137
CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903                                      Data-Over-Cable Service Interface Specifications


15 ADDITIONAL REQUIREMENTS
This section defines the security requirements for DOCSIS 4.0 functionality.

15.1     Increased Security of Keys
15.1.1 Introduction
This service will strengthen the storage and handling of keys in the CM by defining new methods.

15.1.2 Encryption
The CM MUST store any persistently stored copy of the CM device certificate private key using a device-unique
key (DUK) using 128-bit AES encryption or stronger where the clear value of the DUK is not accessible in
software.
A CM MUST NOT allow access through software to any part of the CM device certificate private key.
The CM can do RSA sign and decrypt operations with the CM device certificate private key.

15.1.3 Integrity Protection
Certain parameters are stored in the CM with integrity protection.
The CM MUST store both (a) the cable modem private keys and (b) public keys and certificates in the trust anchor
store inside the CM such that unauthorized modifications are detected using a symmetric keyed hash algorithm that
is equivalent to or stronger than HMAC-SHA256 or alternatively using an asymmetric digital signature algorithm
that is equivalent to or stronger than 2048-bit RSA.
To generate the integrity protection value, the CM MUST use a cryptographic key that is either the CM device
certificate private key or another key that is protected, as well as the CM device certificate private key.
The CM MUST provide integrity protection to all of the CableLabs DOCSIS root CA certificate, CableLabs
DOCSIS root public key, and the manufacturer name used to validate a CVC attribute.

15.2     Secure Cable Modem Boot
15.2.1 Introduction
The requirements defined in this section address primary security goals for the authentication of software
components at cable modem boot time.
Ensuring the authenticity of the operating system and software components executing on top of the cable modem is
vital to the overall operation of DOCSIS-based networks. Executable software authentication is achieved by secure
boot, an attestation process for all software components residing on the cable modem, ensuring these software
components are verified and authenticated.

15.2.2 Secure Boot
The CM authenticates any new software downloaded as defined in Section 14, Secure Software Download.
During the next boot process using secure boot mechanisms, the CM MAY either use the same secure software
download mechanisms, as defined in Section 14, Secure Software Download (SSD), to validate the software to be
booted or implement other techniques that guarantee the same level of protection.
A CM MUST invoke secure boot every time it boots and verify the results attest that software components have not
been tampered with as the operating system code is given control.
A CM MUST apply secure boot requirements to all software components that reside on the CM.




138                                                CableLabs®                                                09/03/25
Security Specification                                                                 CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903


In the case the CM has multiple processors, the CM MUST apply secure boot requirements to all software
components residing on all processors regardless of whether DOCSIS functionality executes on that processor or
not.

15.2.3 Chain of Trust
Upon reset, the CM operates secure boot as a chain process where each step verifies the next step in the chain.
The CM MAY implement the secure boot process in such a way that vendors and service providers are be able to
sign parts of the software components for the CM with different cryptographic keys, creating a chain of trust.
The CM MAY implement the secure boot process such that signing keys in the chain of trust are different from each
other.
The CM signing keys MUST meet the cryptographic requirements that appear in the code verification process
section of this specification.
The CM chain of trust MAY allow different entities (e.g., CM OEM, CM ODM, and service providers) to ensure the
integrity of their respective parts of the software components without sharing signing keys between themselves.
The CM MAY implement a monolithic software image that represents a simplified case of the chain of trust and
multi-processor use cases.

15.2.4 Immutable Root of Trust
An example of immutable root of trust requirements are illustrated in Figure 17.




                                   Figure 17 - Immutable Root of Trust Example


The CM root of trust MUST be immutable.
All CM cryptographic credentials MUST be immutable.
The CM MUST NOT allow its bootloader code to be overridden or changed.
The CM root of trust MAY be unique per manufacture, device model, or network operator.
The CM SHOULD authenticate and verify parameters passed between stages of the secure boot process. For
example, the application processor OS verifies the authenticity of the root file system, and the root file system
includes the OS code that will be loaded into the other processors after authentication.

15.2.5 Code Verification Process
The following requirements apply to the CM code verification process.
    •    All software components that are part of the CM secure boot requirements MUST be digitally signed.




09/03/25                                            CableLabs®                                                      139
CM-SP-SECv4.0-I08-250903                                        Data-Over-Cable Service Interface Specifications


      •   The CM MUST use a hashing algorithm equal or stronger to SHA-256 for digitally signing software
          components.
      •   The CM digital signature MUST be done with one of these options: (a) RSA encryption key modulus that
          are at least 2048 bits in length, (b) elliptic curve encryption with domain parameters that are at least 224
